### OF
# <term>ADONHIRAMITE MASONRY</term><note>Adonhiramite Masonry is a specific branch of Freemasonry that became popular in France in the late 18th century. It centers on the figure of Adonhiram rather than the more common Hiram Abiff.</note>

## FIRST ELECT,
->OR<-
## THE ELECT OF NINE. <note>The "Elect of Nine" is a specific high degree in Freemasonry that tells the story of the nine masters sent by King Solomon to find the killers of the master builder.</note>

---

### Required Decorations.

The Lodge, which represents the Cabinet <note>In this historical context, a "cabinet" refers to a private study or a secret inner chamber of a ruler.</note> of Solomon, must be suitably decorated. The wall hangings may be of several colors. The East <note>The "East" refers to the ceremonial head of the Masonic lodge where the presiding officer sits, symbolizing the source of light and wisdom.</note> must be wide enough to contain two armchairs beneath it. On the same line, at the foot of the throne, to the right, a small altar shall be placed, covered with a cloth, which will hold three yellow wax can-

dles, placed in a square, and the book of wisdom. The lodge must be lit by nine yellow candles suspended in a chandelier, or placed individually in candlesticks on the floor; but there must be one separated from the others by at least one foot. If one wishes to illuminate it further, one may represent the burning bush <note>A biblical reference to the bush that burned but was not consumed, symbolizing the presence of the divine; in a Masonic context, it often represents light and revelation.</note> with leaves and tree branches that shall be lit by small lamps. The floor of the room must be covered with a carpet upon which a child of three or four years shall be placed <note>The presence of a young child in this ritual is a symbolic representation of innocence or the potential for future growth within the order.</note>. Both sides of this carpet contain the seats for the Brothers. 

This Lodge, being the Council of Nine, absolutely cannot be held unless there are nine members present, the first two of whom are Solomon and Hiram, King of Tyre <note>Hiram, King of Tyre, was the Phoenician king who, according to the Bible and Masonic tradition, provided King Solomon with materials and craftsmen to build the Temple in Jerusalem.</note>, who both occupy the throne; but Solomon presides on the right. These two Kings must have crowns on their heads. Solomon's is enriched with precious stones; there shall be none on that of the King of Tyre. He shall only carry a blue gilded scepter, with bands, and surmounted by a luminous triangle, an emblem of wisdom and perfection. The King of Tyre shall hold a large dagger in his hand. The rest of the attire shall be similar to that of the Brothers, except that Solomon shall have gloves trimmed with a frin-

All the Brothers shall be dressed in black and shall wear a small breastplate on the left side, upon which shall be embroidered a skull with a bone and a dagger in saltire <term>in saltire: an heraldic term for items crossed in the shape of an 'X'</term>, in silver; the whole surrounded by the motto, *To conquer or to die* <note>original: "Vaincre ou mourir"</note>. At the bottom of the sash there must be a white ribbon rosette, at the end of which hangs a small dagger in its scabbard. The apron must be of white leather, lined with black; on the bib <note>the triangular top flap of the apron</note> shall be embroidered a skull, with a bone and a sword in saltire, beneath a square <note>the masonic square, a symbol of morality and virtue</note> embroidered in gold. On the pocket of the apron shall be a large tear; at the bottom and on the sides eight other smaller tears; at the end of the pocket a branch of Acacia <note>Acacia is a primary symbol in Freemasonry, representing the immortality of the soul</note>. The gloves shall be lined with black taffeta and bordered with the same.

## TITLES.

The Master is named Most-Wise; the King of Tyre, Most-Powerful; and the Brothers, Most-Respectable. There is no Warden <note>in most Masonic lodges, Wardens are high-ranking officers who assist the Master; their absence here is a unique feature of this specific ritual structure</note>; but the Most-Wise, as soon...

### *Beginning of the Work.*

The Most-Wise <note>the title given to the Master of the Lodge, representing King Solomon</note>, with the crown on his head, being seated, says to the King of Tyre <note>Hiram, the King of Tyre, who provided materials and craftsmen for the Temple</note>, who presents himself at the foot of the Throne: "Most-Powerful King of Tyre, what have you come here to do?" The King of Tyre responds: "Most-Wise, I come to ask of you vengeance for the death of the Architect of the Temple <note>This refers to Hiram Abiff, the master builder whose murder is the central mystery of Masonic legend</note>, which until this day has remained unpunished." The Most-Wise says: "Take your place, my Brother, and be a witness to the searches for the murderer that I am going to order to be made." The King of Tyre ascends <margin>a</margin> to the Throne and takes his place. Solomon names a Brother who, at that instant, comes to place one knee on the ground at the foot of the throne. Solomon takes his scepter and places it upon his head, saying to him: "Brother, I constitute you the Intimate of the Council, to watch over the safety of the Lodge: begin your duties to ensure the qualifications of the Brothers here present." The Brother rises, salutes the two Kings, then goes to take the sign, the grip <term>grip: a specific way of shaking hands used as a secret sign of recognition</term>, and the word <term>word: the secret password of the degree</term> from each Brother. Upon returning to the foot of the Throne, he says: "Most-Wise, the Council has only faithful subjects."

...given inside by the Sentinel Brother <note>The "Frere sentinelle" refers to the Tyler or an inner guard who monitors the door</note>, Solomon appears to be indignant and says with anger: "Intimate Brother, see what is causing this noise, and how my orders are being carried out."

The Brother exits, and returning suddenly with a look of surprise, says: "Thrice-Wise <note>original: "Très-Sage"—the traditional title for the presiding officer in this Masonic degree</note>, the Council is betrayed." All the Brothers put their swords in hand and say: N. N. M. <note>These letters represent the ritual words "Nakam, Nakah, Mahorey," which are Hebrew-derived terms usually translated as "Vengeance! Strike! Death!" in this context</note> Solomon commands authority, saying, with his Scepter raised: "Let our indignation yield for a moment to the necessity of hearing the Intimate Brother’s report. Tell us, Intimate Brother, who caused this rumor, and who has had the audacity to disturb our august Council?"

The Intimate Brother, making the sign of response, says: "I have just seen, with surprise, that a Brother has clandestinely slipped into the exterior of this apartment. It is to be feared that he has heard the secrets of the Council. I will even say, with trembling, that it is to be presumed he is defiled by some great crime: his hands are stained with blood, and the sharp sword <note>original: "glaive"—specifically a ritual sword or broadsword</note> he holds bears witness against him; everything excites my suspicions."

Solomon raises his dagger and says: "May he be sacrificed to the ghost <note>original: "mânes"—the spirits of the dead in Roman mythology, used here to refer to the deceased Master Architect</note> of Adonhiram." The King of Tyre rises and says:

The Intimate Brother, upon arriving, seizes his sword, takes it from him, and sends it to the

<meta>This page continues the ritualistic preparation of the Candidate for initiation. On the previous page, the Intimate Brother (the official in charge of the candidate) had just seized the candidate's sword to send it ahead into the chamber.</meta>

Lodge by a Brother whom he took care to bring with him. This Brother, while presenting it to the Most Wise <note>The "Très-Sage" (Most Wise) is the title for the Master presiding over this specific Masonic body, representing King Solomon</note>, says to him: "He is disarmed."

The Intimate Brother leads him to the end of the Lodge; he passes a red cord or ribbon over his neck, with which his hands are tied, and in which his body is enclosed. Afterward, he is made to take off his shoes entirely; a very thick blindfold is placed over his eyes, and bloody gloves are put on his hands, with his hat removed and his apron worn as a Mason <note>A "Mason's apron" is the primary symbolic garment of the craft; here it is worn in a specific manner to indicate his current standing or the degree he is entering</note>. When he is ready, the Intimate Brother says to him:

> "Examine your heart, my Brother; you are suspected of a great crime, worthy of a punishment capable of terrifying the most ferocious heart. You may, however, hope for indulgence if sincerity guides your words. If you are innocent, follow me with confidence."

Then the Intimate Brother places his dagger over the heart of the Candidate <note>original: "Récipiendaire" — the person receiving the degree</note>, leads him to the door of the Lodge, for which he must have the key. He opens it for the Candidate, introduces him, and places him in the West <note>In Masonic orientation, the "West" is the area furthest from the Master's throne, typically where candidates are first received</note>. When he is there and all the Brothers are seated, Solomon says to the Candidate: "What do you seek?" The Intimate Brother, who dictates the responses for the Candidate, has him say:

> "The reward that is due to me."

--- Page 16 ---
<meta>This page continues the dramatic ritual of the "Elect" degrees. On the previous page, the Candidate was led into the Lodge, blindfolded and wearing bloody gloves, to face an interrogation regarding the murder of the Master Architect.</meta>

**S.** <note>The letter "S" here stands for Solomon, the Master presiding over the ceremony.</note> Do you believe that Masons authorize crime and murder! Rather, tremble at the just punishment reserved for you. Who are you?
**R.** <note>The "R" stands for the Respondent, representing the Candidate speaking through his guide.</note> The best of Masons, the most zealous of all the Brothers, or at least the one most worthy of that title.
**S.** Vile assassin! What do you dare to say when you present yourself in this sacred place with your hands stained with blood that is undoubtedly innocent? Everything testifies against you; everything announces a murder.
**R.** I submit myself to everything if I am guilty.
**The King of Tyre:** <note>Hiram, King of Tyre, was Solomon's ally who provided materials and craftsmen for the Temple.</note> Let Adonhiram <note>Also known as Hiram Abiff, the legendary chief architect of Solomon's Temple whose murder is a central theme in Masonic mythology.</note> be avenged.
All the Brothers respond: N. N. M. <note>These initials likely refer to the Hebrew word "Nekam" or "Nekum" (Vengeance), a common password in the "Elect" or "Vengeance" degrees.</note>
**The King of Tyre:** My Brothers, be glad; the murderer of Adonhiram has been discovered.
**S.** The deception is too crude, since he seeks to trick us. How do you respond?
**R.** That I am wrongly suspected of the death of a Master whose memory I respect. I come only with the intent of giving you news of it through the discoveries I have made.
**S.** What is this news?
**R.** A cave, a burning bush, a gushing fountain, a dog for a guide, <note>These are specific symbols of the "Elect of Nine" degree, where a dog leads the seeker to a cavern where one of the assassins is hiding near a spring.</note>

<summary>In this ritual dialogue, the Candidate is accused of murdering the Master Architect Adonhiram, but he defends his innocence by claiming he has discovered the true location of the assassin.</summary>

**S.** What are the guarantees of this?

**R.** My hands, soaked in the blood of three animals—the lion, the tiger, and the bear—which he had tamed to guard the entrance to his cave, and which I destroyed to reach it. <note>In this degree's mythology, these animals represent the passions or obstacles that a Mason must overcome to reach the truth.</note>

**S.** What have you come to ask?

**R.** To throw myself at the King’s feet to receive his orders, and to know if he wishes me to deliver Abiram <note>Abiram is the name given in this tradition to the chief assassin of Hiram Abiff.</note> to him, dead or alive.

**S.** What proof do you give us of your faith?

**R.** The most sacred promises shall be the guarantees of my innocence, and the most rigorous punishments to which I consent to be exposed if I am found to be a criminal.

**S.** <term>Intimate Brother</term> <note>original: "Frere Intime"; an officer in the Lodge acting as a master of ceremonies or guard.</note>, since this Brother is beginning to calm our suspicions, have him advance by nine steps—three of an <term>Apprentice</term> <note>original: "Apprentif"; the 1st degree.</note>, three of a <term>Fellowcraft</term> <note>original: "Compagnon"; the 2nd degree.</note>, and three of a <term>Master</term> <note>original: "Maître"; the 3rd degree.</note>—as far as the throne, to come here and take his obligation in our hands.

...compasses which embrace a mallet. <note>In this ritual context, the mallet represents the tool of the architect, and the compasses represent the limits of one's actions; together they symbolize the discipline of the craft.</note> Solomon places his dagger on his forehead, and the <term>Intimate Brother</term> <note>original: "Frere intime"; an officer of the lodge who assists in the ritual.</note> places a naked sword on his back; then the <term>Most Wise</term> <note>original: "Très-Sage"; the title given to the presiding Master of the lodge in this degree.</note>, striking a blow of his scepter on the <term>Altar</term>, so that all the Brothers may rise, says to him: "Take heed to what you are about to do. The moment is critical; if you seek to deceive us, our indulgence will increase the rigor of the punishments that will follow. If you are sincere, pronounce with us:

### O B L I G A T I O N .

> "I promise, on the word of an honest man, and before this august Assembly, at the feet of the highest Power of <term>Masonry</term>, never to reveal to any man who has not done what I have done, the secrets that allow one to reach and give the sublime title of <term>Elected Master</term> <note>original: "Maître-Elu"; the 9th degree in several French Masonic systems.</note>. I promise to scrupulously fulfill its obligations, at the peril of my blood, in whatever encounter it may be, to sacrifice to the <term>manes</term> <note>original: "mânes"; the spirits or ghosts of the ancestors/dead.</note> of <term>Adoniram</term> <note>original: "Adonhiram"; in some French rites, he is the central architect figure instead of or in addition to Hiram Abiff.</note> the perjurers who might reveal any of our secrets to the <term>Profanes</term> <note>original: "Profanes"; those who have not been initiated into the mysteries of Masonry.</note>. I will keep my engagements, or let the most frightful death be the expiation of my per- <note>The text breaks here; the word is likely "parjure" (perjury).</note>

### *Catechism of the First Elect.* <note>A "Catechism" in this context is a scripted series of questions and answers used to teach and test a member's knowledge of the ritual's secrets and story.</note>

Q. Are you an <term>Elect Master</term> <note>This refers to the "Elect of Nine," a degree focused on the biblical/legendary search for the killers of the master builder of Solomon's Temple.</note>?
A. Yes, <term>Most Wise</term> <note>The "Très-Sage" is the title of the presiding officer of the lodge in this degree, representing King Solomon.</note>, I am.
Q. By what shall I know it?
A. By the sign, the touch, and the word.
Q. Give them?
A. He gives them. <note>The stage directions indicate the candidate performs the secret gestures and passwords described on the previous page.</note>
Q. Where were you received as an Elect Master?
A. In the Hall of <term>Solomon</term> <note>Referring to the legendary setting of the ritual in King Solomon’s Temple.</note>.
Q. What motive led you to solicit this
title?
A. The desire to avenge the death of <term>Adonhiram</term> <note>In many French Masonic rites, Adonhiram is the name used for Hiram Abiff, the master architect who was murdered by three unfaithful workmen.</note>.
Q. Who was the murderer of Adonhiram?
A. <term>Abiram</term> <note>The name given to the primary assassin in this specific ritual tradition; his name is explained here as meaning "murderer."</note>, whose name signifies mur-
derer or assassin.
Q. How did you arrive at the place of
vengeance?

<summary>This page concludes the initiation of an Elect Master and begins the formal catechism explaining the degree's mythology, specifically the quest to avenge the murder of Adonhiram (Hiram Abiff) by the assassin Abiram.</summary>
<keywords>Elect Master, Catechism, Solomon, Adonhiram, Abiram, vengeance ritual, Masonic degree, Most Wise</keywords>

--- Page 29 ---
<meta>Continuing the catechism from page 22, this section details the symbolic journey of the "Elect of Nine" to find the murderer of the Master Architect.</meta>

<page-num>23</page-num>

A. By dark and unknown paths.
Q. Who led you there?
A. A stranger.
Q. Where was the place of vengeance located?
A. At the foot of a **burning bush** <note>The burning bush is a potent biblical symbol of divine presence, here used to mark the location of the traitor's hiding place.</note> in a dark cave.
Q. What did you find in this cavern?
A. The traitor <term>Abiram</term> <note>In this Masonic legend, Abiram is the name given to the primary assassin of the Master Architect, Adonhiram.</note>, a fountain, a light, and a dagger.
Q. What was the use of all that?
A. The light enlightened me, the fountain quenched my thirst, and the dagger was reserved to avenge the death of Adonhiram, by the blow that Abiram received, which caused him to fall dead on the spot.
Q. Did this wretch say any word?
A. Yes, he said one, but I cannot utter it.
Q. Tell me only the first word, and I will tell you the second?
A. Vengeance <note>original: "Nekar", a Hebrew-derived word used as a password in this degree.</note>.
Q. Revenged <note>original: "Nekum", the second part of the ritual password, also of Hebrew origin.</note>. What did you do with the body of Abiram?
A. I cut off his head, which I carried to Solomon, to inform him that the vengeance was accomplished.
Q. What time was it when you arrived?
A. The break of day.
Q. How many <term>Elect Masters</term> <note>The "Elect of Nine" refers to the nine masters chosen by Solomon to hunt down the assassins.</note> were there for this vengeance?

<summary>This page continues the "Elect of Nine" catechism, describing the symbolic discovery and punishment of the traitor Abiram in a cave. It explains the ritual significance of the spring, the light, and the dagger used to avenge the Master Architect.</summary>
<keywords>Elect of Nine, Abiram, Adonhiram, Solomon, Masonic ritual, vengeance, Nekar, Nekum</keywords>

--- Page 30 ---
<meta>This page concludes the catechism of the Elect of Nine degree, picking up from the question at the end of page 23 regarding the number of masters involved in the pursuit of the traitor.</meta>

A. Nine.
Q. What remains for you to do?
A. Nothing, for all is accomplished.
Q. What time is it?
A. The onset of night, the hour at which I emerged from the cavern.
Q. What is the password?
A. Sserkin <note>original: "Sserkin". This is a ritual password, likely a phonetic variation of a Hebrew term used in the "Elect" degrees of Freemasonry.</note>.

Solomon:
> My Brothers, may such a memorable
> hour be always present in our
> minds, and constantly remind us of the
> zeal of the nine Masters, so that we may imitate them.

Solomon strikes seven equal blows upon the Altar, and Hiram strikes the last two in quick succession, after which the Most Wise <note>The "Most Wise" (Très-Sage) is the title given to the presiding officer in this specific degree.</note> says:
> "My Brothers, the vengeance
> is accomplished; the Council may
> retire; the Lodge of the Elect Masters is
> closed."

All the Brothers strike nine claps with their hands—seven equal, two quick—and the acclamations <note>Ritualistic cheers or chants performed at the opening or closing of a Masonic meeting.</note> are made.

*NOTE.* When the Lodge assembles, and all the Brothers are dressed, with the exception of the <term>sash</term> <note>original: "cordon". A ceremonial ribbon or sash worn as part of the Masonic regalia; in this degree, it is black to symbolize mourning and solemnity.</note> which they must place over the left arm, the Most Wise places himself at the foot of the Altar and puts the black sash on all the Brothers, one after another, taking care to have each one kiss it individually before placing it around the neck.

<summary>This page concludes the ritual of the Elect of Nine, featuring the final questions of the catechism and the formal closing of the Lodge by Solomon and Hiram. It also describes the ceremonial distribution of the black sash, a key piece of regalia for this degree.</summary>
<keywords>Elect of Nine, Solomon, Hiram, Most Wise, password, Sserkin, ritual closing, black sash, Masonic regalia</keywords>

--- Page 31 ---
<meta>This page continues the instructional portion of the ritual for the Elect Masters (Maitres Elus) degree, moving from the closing of the formal Lodge ceremony to the specific protocols for the ceremonial meal, or Table Lodge.</meta>

( 25 )

Elect Masters must never be found in any Lodge, whether inferior <note>Inferior lodges refer to those working in the "Blue" degrees of Entered Apprentice, Fellowcraft, or Master Mason.</note> or superior, without wearing their black **sash** <note>original: "cordon". A ritual ribbon or stole worn as part of the regalia.</note> and their **dagger**, although they must only make use of the latter in Lodges of the Elect.

---

### *Table Lodge.*

The Table Lodge is held like the others, with the exception that there is no **Warden**; instead, the **Intimate Brother**, placed opposite the **two Kings** <note>Reference to King Solomon and King Hiram of Tyre, the two presiding figures in this ritual's mythology.</note>, performs that function. The Lodge is opened with nine knocks; then some questions from the **Catechism** <note>The set of ritualized questions and answers used to test a member's knowledge of the degree.</note> are asked, and it is announced that the Lodge of the Elect is open.

When toasts <note>original: "santés". These are ceremonial drinks shared by the members.</note> are proposed, the Brothers draw their daggers and place them across their <term>glass</term> <note>original: "canon" (cannon). In Masonic dining vocabulary, glasses are called cannons, bottles are casks, and wine is powder.</note>; the dagger is then withdrawn and placed beside the glass. The toast is drunk in the usual manner, and the dagger is immediately placed back onto the glass in three movements without making a sound. The one commanding the toast makes the **sign of request**, and all the Brothers make the **sign of response**; the same is done when one wishes to ask for the floor to speak: knives are referred to as "daggers."

<sig>*Volume II.* C</sig>

<summary>This page outlines the dress code for Elect Masters and provides the specific ritual protocols for conducting a Table Lodge, including the symbolic use of daggers during toasts.</summary>
<keywords>Elect Masters, Table Lodge, Masonic regalia, ritual toasts, Intimate Brother, Masonic vocabulary, ceremonial daggers</keywords>

<sig>C</sig>

When holding a **Lodge of the Elect** <note>original: "Loge d'Elu". One of the "High Degrees" of Masonry that expands upon the story of the Master Mason degree.</note> and there is an **Initiation** <note>original: "Réception". The formal ceremony of admitting a new member into a degree.</note>, the Lodge is opened in the degree of **Master** <note>The third and final degree of basic "Blue Lodge" Freemasonry.</note>. The **Candidate** <note>original: "Récipiendaire".</note> is present for this. Once the Lodge is open, the **Worshipful Master** <note>original: "le Respectable". The title used for the presiding officer of the lodge.</note> has the Candidate sit at the foot of the **Altar**. All the **Brothers** sit on seats brought behind them; then the Worshipful Master exhorts them to listen attentively to the **Discourse** that will be delivered to them, which concerns their initiation. Once the Discourse is finished, the Candidates are made to stand and are sent to the **Chamber of Reflection** <note>A small, secluded, and often dimly lit room where a candidate is left to meditate on their life and mortality before the initiation ceremony begins.</note>; the Master’s Lodge is closed, and the Lodge of the Elect is opened, as described at the beginning of this **Collection** <note>original: "Recueil". A reference to this specific manuscript or book of rituals.</note>.

# SECOND ELECT,
### CALLED
# ELECT OF PÉRIGNAN.

---

### *Arrangement of the Lodge.*

THE decoration is the same as in the previous **Degree** <note>The "First Elect" or "Elect of Nine," which initiates the story of the hunt for the Master's assassins.</note>, except that the **Lodge** is lit by 27 lights, arranged in three groups of 9. The **Most Worshipful** <note>original: "Très-Respectable". The title for the presiding officer in this degree.</note>, seated on the throne, strikes a single loud blow and says:

> ”Brother **Wardens** <note>The two officers ranking just below the Worshipful Master, who help oversee the lodge.</note>, see that we are properly **tiled** <note>original: "couverts". A Masonic term meaning the door is guarded against intruders or non-members.</note> and that we are in safety.”

After the Wardens have responded, the Worshipful Master asks the following questions:
Q. Venerable First Warden, do you know, in your capacity as an Elect, any other mysteries than those of the letters **N. N. M.**? <note>These initials stand for Hebrew words—Nekam, Nakah, and Maka—which translate to "Vengeance," "To strike," and "Death/Plague," referencing the pursuit of the traitors.</note>
A. Yes. I know the letter **P**.
Q. What does this letter signify?
A. It is the initial of the name of the unknown man who warned **Solomon** <note>King Solomon, who in Masonic legend presided over the building of the Temple in Jerusalem.</note> of the hiding place of **Abiram** <note>Also known as Akirop or Johaben; in the legend, he is one of the three "ruffians" who murdered the master architect Hiram Abiff.</note>, and who offered to lead the nine Elect Masters there.

<summary>This page introduces the Second Elect degree, also known as the Elect of Pérignan, detailing the ritual arrangement of the lodge and the symbolic meaning of the letter "P." It establishes the role of Pérignan as the guide who led the search for the murderer of the master architect.</summary>
<keywords>Second Elect, Elect of Pérignan, Masonic ritual, Wardens, King Solomon, Abiram, Hiram Abiff, Vengeance, 27 lights</keywords>

**Q.** At what hour does the **Lodge** <term>Loge: The meeting place and the organized body of Freemasons.</term> of this **Degree** <term>Grade: A level of membership or rank within the Masonic system.</term> open?
**A.** At the onset of night or at the decline of day.
**Q.** What time is it?
**A.** The day is finished.

The **Most Worshipful** <note>original: "Très-Respectable". The presiding officer of the Lodge.</note> then strikes twenty-seven blows in three sets of nine, makes the **sign** <note>A specific physical gesture associated with this degree.</note>, and says: ”Venerable **Senior Warden** <note>original: "premier Surveillant". The officer second in command.</note>, do your duty by announcing upon your **columns** <note>original: "colonnes". This refers to the rows of members seated in the north and south of the room, overseen by the Wardens.</note> that the Lodge of the Second **Elect** <term>Elu: A title given to members of these degrees, referencing the "chosen" masters sent to find Hiram's killers.</term> is open.” This he executes. After which, the usual acclamations are made. Then the Most Worshipful says: ”Venerable Senior and Junior Wardens, inquire upon your columns if any Elect has anything to propose.” They do so.

Then the **Master of Ceremonies** rises and says: ”There is an Elect Master of the First Degree, who wishes to know the mysteries of the Second.” The Most Worshipful asks if the **ballot** <note>original: "scrutin". A vote by the members to ensure the candidate is worthy of advancement.</note> was favorable to him at the previous assembly; and then he orders the Master of Ceremonies to introduce him, after having examined him on the Degrees he possesses, and especially on the First Elect. This examination completed, he leads the **Candidate** <note>original: "Récipiendaire". The person receiving the degree.</note>, with all his clothes and his **sash** <note>original: "cordon". A decorative silk ribbon worn over the shoulder as part of Masonic regalia.</note>, and places him between

... the two **Wardens** <term>Surveillans: Officers who assist the Master in governing the Lodge; there is a Senior Warden and a Junior Warden.</term>. The **Most Worshipful** <note>original: "Très-Respectable". The title for the presiding officer of the Lodge.</note> asks him what he desires. To which he responds: ”To know the Grade of Second **Elect** <term>Elu: A "high degree" in Masonry where members represent the chosen masters sent by King Solomon to find the assassins of the master builder Hiram Abiff.</term>.”

**Q.** My Brothers, do you believe him worthy of it?
**A.** The **Junior Warden** <note>original: "second Surveillant".</note> and the **Master of Ceremonies** respond: Yes.

The **Candidate** <term>Récipiendaire: The person receiving the degree or being initiated.</term> travels across the four cardinal points twice; that is to say, starting from the **West** <note>Occident</note> where he is located, he ascends to the **East** <note>Orient</note> by way of the **South** <note>Midi</note>, and descends again to the **West** by the same path. There, he travels twice through the **West**, coming and going. He does the same in the **East**, then in the **North** <note>Septentrion</note>, and returns afterward to resume his place, from where he crosses the **Lodge** <term>Loge: Both the physical room and the assembled body of Masons.</term> to go and take his **Obligation** <term>Obligation: A formal and binding vow of secrecy and loyalty.</term>. This crossing makes up the nine journeys. It must be noted that while making them, one salutes the throne nine times.

### O B L I G A T I O N .

> ”I swear and promise, on my word of honor, the faith of an honest man, in the presence of the **Grand Architect of the Universe** <note>A non-denominational term used by Freemasons to refer to God.</note>, and before this Assembly, to keep and observe the mysteries of the second Grade of **Elect** which are about to be entrusted to me, not only towards the **Profane** <note>Profanes: Those who are not members of the Masonic order.</note>, but also...

...more toward the **Brothers** <term>Freres: Members of the Masonic fraternity.</term> who are in the **Grades** <term>Grades: Degrees or levels of initiation within Freemasonry.</term> inferior to this one; all under the penalties set forth by my first **Obligation**; consenting furthermore to have my tongue torn out and to be considered infamous <note>In historical Masonic rituals, "infamous" (infâme) referred to someone who had lost their legal standing and social honor due to a breach of their word.</note>, from which may God preserve me, and may He be my help. Amen.

## Catechism of the second *Elect.*

**Q.** Do you know any other **Elect** <term>Elu: A title given to a Mason who has attained this specific "high degree" involving the legend of King Solomon's chosen masters.</term> than the one of the letters N. N. M.? <note>Original: "N. N. M." usually stands for *Neuf Neuf Maîtres* (Nine, Nine Masters) or *Nos Neuf Maîtres* (Our Nine Masters), referring to the legendary search party.</note>
**A.** Yes, I know the letter **P.**
**Q.** What does this letter signify?
**A.** It is the initial of the name of the stranger who came to reveal to **Solomon** the retreat of **Abiram** <note>In this ritual tradition, Abiram (or Akirop) is the name given to the primary assassin of Hiram Abiff, the master architect of Solomon's Temple.</note>.
**Q.** Pronounce his name in full?
**A.** **Pérignan**, after whom this Grade is named.
**Q.** How were you introduced into the **Lodge**?
**A.** By 27 knocks struck at different intervals, nine at a time.
**Q.** What do these knocks, thus repeated, signify?
**A.** Three things. The first, that I was one of the nine **Elects** who went in search of the assassin, or at least that I desired to be one of them. The second, the nine **Masters** who went in search of the

<keywords>Second Elect, Pérignan, Abiram, Solomon, ritual knocks, Masonic oath, catechism, Nine Masters</keywords>

corpse of our dear Master **Adonhiram**. The third [significance], the blows that were struck at the three doors by the three false **Brothers**.

**Q.** What do these three letters **R. G. A.** signify, which are in the floor plan <term>Tracé: A symbolic diagram or "tracing board" drawn or placed on the floor of the Lodge to illustrate the teachings of a specific degree.</term> of the Lodge?
**A.** The names of the assassins of our Respectable Master **Adonhiram**.
**Q.** Name them?
**A.** **Romvel**, **Gravelot**, **Abiram** <note>These specific names—Romvel, Gravelot, and Abiram—are traditional to early French Masonic "high degrees." They are variant names for the three "ruffians" who, in the standard legend, murder the Master Architect.</note>.
**Q.** How did these three wretches position themselves to carry out their detestable crime?
**A.** **Romvel** at the West gate, armed with a rule; **Gravelot** at the North gate, armed with a mallet; and **Abiram** at the South gate, armed with a lever. It was he who knocked him to the ground and left him dead.
**Q.** What was the fate of **Romvel** and **Gravelot**? Our first **Elect** <term>Premier Elu: Refers to the "Elect of Nine," the preceding degree in this rite, which focuses solely on the capture of the first assassin.</term> only teaches us about that of **Abiram**.
**A.** **Solomon** discovered that they had perished miserably in the land of **Cabul** <note>Original: "pays Cabule." In the Bible, Cabul is a region given by Solomon to King Hiram of Tyre; in this ritual's mythology, it serves as the site where the remaining assassins met their end.</note>, where they had taken refuge.
**Q.** How did **Pérignan**—that stranger from the first grade—know that **Abiram** had taken refuge in his cave?
**A.** **Pérignan**, while working at a bush at the foot of which the cave was located, his dog

began to bark. He looked and saw a man enter, looking completely terrified. His curiosity led him to find out who he was. Abiram, seeing himself discovered by this stranger, threw himself at his feet, confided his secret to him, begging him not to reveal it to Solomon, kissed his hands to move him to pity, and begged him to help him in the urgent hunger that was devouring him.

**Q.** Why did the stranger warn Solomon?
**A.** To satisfy the Decree <term>Edit: A formal royal proclamation or law.</term> issued by this wise King and to obtain the reward for it.
**Q.** How long did **Pérignan** feed **Abiram** before revealing him to **Solomon**?
**A.** Seven full days.
**Q.** Why did he delay so long in declaring him?
**A.** Because he only learned of the King's Decree seven days after the discovery, while going to the city to find food for himself and for **Abiram**.
**Q.** What is the sacred word <term>Mot sacré: A specific password used by members to recognize each other within this degree of masonry.</term> of this Grade?
**A.** **Moabon** <note>original: "Moabon". While the etymology of this word in Masonry is often debated, this specific ritual provides its own internal definition in the following answer.</note>.
**Q.** What does this word mean?
**A.** Praised be God that the crime and the criminal are punished.
**Q.** What is the sign <term>Signe: A ritual gesture used for recognition or to convey a symbolic meaning.</term> of this Grade?
**A.** It is to pretend to tear out one's tongue while extending the hands.
**Q.** What is the response to this sign?

<meta>This page continues the "Catechism" (instructional Q&A) for the degree of Second Elect, or the Grade of Pérignan. It follows the previous page's explanation of why the stranger (Pérignan) reported the assassin Abiram to King Solomon after hiding him for seven days.</meta>

**Q.** From where do you derive this sign?
**A.** Partly from my obligation <term>Obligation: A solemn oath taken by a candidate during a Masonic initiation ceremony.</term>, and partly from the surprise felt by Abiram upon seeing himself discovered.
**Q.** What is your token <term>Attouchement: A specific physical touch or grip used by members to recognize each other's rank or degree.</term>?
**A.** To present the hand to the one whom one wishes to recognize, who must then take it and kiss it.
**Q.** To what does this token relate?
**A.** To the kissing of the hand that Abiram performed for Pérignan to induce him to remain silent.
**Q.** What is the password?
**A.** **Abiram**, which means murderer or assassin. <note>original: "Abiram". In the context of this ritual, the name is used as a mnemonic for the crime itself.</note>
**Q.** What became of the head of this wretch?
**A.** It was embalmed, and Solomon had it placed on the end of a pike, with a dagger beneath it, placed crosswise <term>En sautoir: A heraldic and ritual term meaning two objects crossed in the shape of an 'X'.</term>, and displayed at the North <term>Septentrion: An archaic term for the North, referring to the seven stars of the Big Dipper.</term> of the Temple, to show that crime does not remain unpunished.
**Q.** What is signified by the nine circuits <note>original: "tours". These are ritualistic laps or "perambulations" walked by a candidate during an initiation.</note> you made during your journey, before taking your obligation?
**A.** The nine days that Abiram remained hidden in the cave.

## D I S C O U R S E.

You have finally attained the Rank of Second Elect, Worshipful Master <term>Worshipful Master: Though usually the title of the presiding officer of a lodge, in this context it is used as an honorary address for the candidate who has completed the degree's requirements.</term>, to this Rank, which has long been the object of your desires, and which your zeal and your labors have procured for you; permit me to congratulate you upon it. We have conferred it upon you from the heart, and all the more willingly, as this favor will undoubtedly make you worthy, through your efforts, of penetrating the sublime mysteries which remain for us to discover to you; you learned, in the first Rank of Elect <term>First Elect: Also known as the Elect of Nine, the previous degree focused on the discovery and punishment of the first assassin, Abiram, in a cavern.</term>, the fate of the treacherous Abiram; and the second instructs you on the fatal end of the two other villains, Romvel and Gravelot <note>In the Adonhiramite Rite, the three assassins of the master architect Hiram Abiff are named Abiram, Romvel, and Gravelot. In other Masonic traditions, they are often called Jubela, Jubelo, and Jubelum.</note>, who, after having long wandered from Province to Province, dragging everywhere the remorse of their crime, perished miserably. A just effect of divine vengeance, which never leaves crime without punishment. The entire allegory contained in this new Rank is very easy to develop; you will find it all laid out in this tracing board <note>original: "tableau". A "tracing board" is a symbolic illustration used in Masonic lodges to depict the emblems, characters, and moral lessons of a specific degree.</note> which offers itself to your eyes, and of which the principal and most striking object of horror and terror to all those who continue to work on the Temple,

Volume II. D

# THIRD ELECT,
### NAMED
## ELECT OF THE FIFTEEN.

---

->*Arrangement of the Lodge.*<-

THE Lodge must be hung with black and scattered with red and white tears. <note>In Masonic iconography, "tears" are symbolic droplets painted or embroidered onto funeral hangings to represent mourning; red tears typically represent blood, and white tears represent sorrow.</note> In the East, there must be a skeleton, representing the traitor Abiram, following the Degree of the Elect of Nine, whose true name is Hoben: <note>Masonic legends often assign specific names to the three "ruffians" who murdered Hiram Abiff. "Hoben" or "Abiram" is usually the first discovered.</note> in the West, on the North side, there is another skeleton representing Oterfut; in the East, on the South side, another skeleton representing Sterkin; <note>The placement of these skeletons creates a symbolic landscape of the crime and its punishment.</note> each of whom must be armed with the fatal instrument with which the murderers struck our Respectable Master. <term>Respectable Master: Here referring to Hiram Abiff, the central architectural figure of Masonic legend whose death the Elect degrees seek to avenge.</term>

The Lodge is opened by five knocks repeated three times. <note>This total of fifteen knocks corresponds to the name of the degree: Elect of the Fifteen.</note> The Master having knocked, the five lights placed in the East, on the left, are lit. Then the Senior Warden <term>Senior Warden: The "Premier Surveillant," the officer second in command, usually situated in the West.</term> knocks in the same manner; the five other lights are lit; and the Junior Warden <term>Junior Warden: The "Second Surveillant," the third-ranking officer, situated in the South.</term> having knocked, the five [remaining]...

All respond: "Yes, Most Respectable Master." "Have him approach the throne," says the Grand Master. &nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;After the obligation, the history of the other murderers of *Adonhiram* <note>In some Masonic traditions, Adonhiram is identified as the architect of the Temple, distinct from or a variation of Hiram Abiff.</note> is recounted. *My Dearest Brother*, you learned in the Grade of *Elect Master of the Nine*, through which you have already passed, that *Abiram*—killed in the cavern beneath the burning bush—was a murderer of *Adonhiram*. ## S I G N S.

The first is to close the right hand, the thumb raised as if holding a dagger, bringing it under the chin, and moving it down along the body, as if wishing to cut oneself open. The second, which is the response of the Examiner, is to extend the hand, pretending to cut one's throat with the thumb.
The sacred word is *Zéomet*; and the response is *Eléham*, which will also serve as the password to enter the Temple.
The first grip <note>The "attouchement" or touch is a secret physical sign, such as a handshake, used to recognize fellow members.</note> is to give two small taps with the index finger on the joint of the little finger, which alludes to the two traitors discovered <note>Referring to the assassins Sterkin and Oterfut mentioned on the previous page.</note>. The second is to take the right hand of the Examiner with the five fingers of the right hand extended; which signifies three times five, and consequently the number of fifteen <term>Elect</term> <note>The "Elect of the Fifteen" refers to the fifteen master masons chosen by Solomon to search for the murderers of the architect.</note>.
The Sash of the Grand Master Elect is of...

### *Manner of introducing the Candidate.*

<margin><unclear>u</unclear> <unclear>oh</unclear></margin>
He must be dressed as an <term>Elect of the Nine</term> <note>The Masonic degree immediately preceding this one; its ritual centers on the initial discovery of the first assassin.</note>, and hold two skulls, as stated above. When there is no Reception <note>The formal ceremony of initiating a new member into the degree.</note>, the Lodge opens as follows, with the exception that when the fifteen lights are lit, the Grand Master says: "Assist me in opening the Lodge." Then all the Brethren, standing and with heads uncovered, make the same sign.

Q. What time is it?
A. Five o'clock in the evening.
Q. Why?
A. Because it was at this hour that the murderers of Adonhiram <note>In this tradition, Adonhiram is the architect of Solomon's Temple whose death drives the narrative of the "Elect" degrees.</note> were discovered and seized to be brought to Jerusalem.
Q. Have they been discovered?
A. Yes, <term>Most Worshipful</term> <note>A traditional honorific title used for the Master of a Masonic Lodge.</note>, they have been and were presented to Solomon.

"My Brethren," says the Grand Master, "since these two murderers have been discovered and arrested, let us place ourselves..."

**Q.** Are you a Grand Master Elect?
**A.** Yes, my zeal and my labor have procured this <term>Rank</term> <note>original: "Grade"</note> for me, along with the esteem of my Superiors.

**Q.** When did he receive you?
**A.** When he sent me with my Brethren to seek the two last assassins of Adonhiram.

**Q.** What do these three heads signify?
**A.** Those of the three assassins of Adonhiram.
**Q.** You say that you went in search of two?

Tome II. E

The Lodge is closed. They strike fifteen knocks, in three sets of five.

### Design of the Apron.

It is made of white skin, bordered with a black ribbon. In the middle, there must be a tower embroidered in silver; three rosettes of black ribbon—one at each corner and one on the flap—which signify the three heads; below the flap shall be placed **H**; below the rosette on the left, **O**; and on the right, **S**. <note>The letters H, O, and S refer to the three assassins of the master architect: Hoben, Oterfut, and Sterkin.</note>

## Catechism of the Perfect Elect.

**Q.** Are you an Elect?
**A.** I am.
**Q.** What is the work of the Perfect Elect?
**A.** To rectify morals. <note>This marks a shift in the ritual's focus from physical vengeance against the ruffians to the internal moral improvement of the Mason.</note>
**Q.** Where do the Perfect Elect travel?
**A.** To the sovereign vault. <note>The "sovereign vault" or "sacred vault" refers to the secret underground chamber where, according to legend, the lost Word of the Masons was hidden or rediscovered.</note>
**Q.** Through what did you pass?
**A.** Through a long corridor lit by 3, 5, 7, and 9. <note>These numbers are highly symbolic in Masonry, often representing the stages of life, the senses, the liberal arts, and the steps of the winding staircase.</note>
**Q.** What does the number 3 signify?

<summary>This page describes the ritual regalia for the Elect degree and begins the catechism for the "Perfect Elect," focusing on moral rectification and the journey to a secret vault.</summary>
<keywords>Adonhiram, Perfect Elect, Freemasonry, Apron, Catechism, Sovereign Vault, Hoben, Oterfut, Sterkin</keywords>

--- Page 56 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Catechism of the Perfect Elect," following the symbolic numerical sequence of 3, 5, 7, and 9 mentioned at the bottom of page 49.</meta>

**A.** The three principal columns: **F**, **S**, and **B**; <note>These initials represent *Force* (Strength), *Sagesse* (Wisdom), and *Beauté* (Beauty), the three symbolic pillars that support a Masonic Lodge.</note> the age of an Apprentice. <note>In Masonic ritual, "age" refers to symbolic seniority rather than chronological years; an Apprentice is symbolically three years old.</note>
**Q.** What does the number 5 signify?
**A.** The five orders of Architecture: the Ionic, the Doric, the Tuscan, the Corinthian, and the Composite; the age of a Fellowcraft. <note>The Fellowcraft degree traditionally focuses on the liberal arts and sciences, including architecture.</note>
**Q.** What does the number 9 signify?
**A.** The nine Masters; the perfect age of a Mason.
**Q.** What did you find before the corridor?
**A.** A respectable Elect who asked me for the password.
**Q.** How did you give it to him?
**A.** Chibot, <note>original: "Chibot." This is a variant spelling of a password often appearing in high-degree French Masonry, likely related to "Shibboleth" or "Giblim."</note> three times.
**Q.** What does the sacred vault contain?
**A.** The Word.
**Q.** What is this Word?
**A.** That which was lost in the ruins of the Temple. <note>The "Lost Word" is a central motif in Masonic mythology, representing a divine secret or a master's recognition key that was lost upon the death of the master architect.</note>
**Q.** To whom was this Word first given?
**A.** To Moses in a burning bush.
**Q.** To whom was it transmitted?
**A.** To the Perfect Elect alone.
**Q.** What was this Word used for?
**A.** By those who were searching for the murderers of Adonhiram.
**Q.** Give it to me?
**A.** I cannot, Most Respectable. <note>The presiding officer of the Lodge in this degree is addressed as "Most Respectable." The refusal to give the Word is a standard ritual test of the candidate's secrecy.</note>

<summary>This page concludes the numerical symbolism of the Perfect Elect degree and describes the discovery of the "Lost Word" within a sacred vault, tracing its lineage back to Moses.</summary>
<keywords>numerical symbolism, orders of architecture, sacred vault, Lost Word, Moses, Adonhiram, password, Masonic age</keywords>

--- Page 57 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Catechism of the Perfect Elect," picking up directly from the search for the murderers mentioned on the previous page. The "Most Respectable" mentioned in the text is the title for the presiding Master of the Lodge in this degree.</meta>

**Q.** Have you found the murderer?
**A.** I have punished him.
**Q.** Where did you find him?
**A.** In the dark cavern, near the fountain of Siloam. <note>The Fountain of Siloam is a biblical site in Jerusalem; in Masonic legend, it is the location where one of the assassins of the Master Architect was discovered hiding.</note>
**Q.** In what posture was he?
**A.** In this one, Most Respectable. (*Making the gesture.*)
**Q.** What does this posture signify?
**A.** Remorse and sadness.
**Q.** What was his name?
**A.** Abiram. <note>Abiram (or Akirop) is the traditional name given in the "Elect" degrees to one of the three ruffians who murdered the Master Architect, Adonhiram.</note>
**Q.** What are the tools of the Perfect Elect?
**A.** The Hammer, the Shovel, and the Crowbar. <note>These tools represent the physical act of discovering the body of the Master or the pursuit of the assassins.</note>
**Q.** Give me the grip?
**A.** I obey, saying: N. N. M. <note>original: "N. N. M." These initials usually stand for the Hebrew words *Nekam* (Vengeance), *Nekah* (Smitten), and *Maka* (Death/Wound), reflecting the theme of the degree.</note> which he pronounces.
**Q.** Give me the explanation of N. N. M.?
**A.** Perfect.
**Q.** Give me the word of vengeance?
**A.** Sterkin. <note>original: "Sterkin." This is a symbolic word in the Elect degrees, likely derived from a Hebrew term relating to decay or the discovery of the body.</note>
**Q.** What is your age?
**A.** Nine years, Most Respectable. <note>In Masonry, "age" refers to symbolic progression; nine is the specific number associated with the Elect degrees, often representing the nine masters sent to find the assassins.</note>
**Q.** Are the sun's rays shining upon us?
**A.** Our Respectable Master is avenged.
**Q.** What is the password?
**A.** Berith-Neder Aliam. <note>original: "Bérit-Neder Aliam." These are likely phonetic renderings of Hebrew: *Berith* (Covenant), *Neder* (Vow), and *Aliam* (possibly a corruption of *Elion*, "the Most High," or *Alm*, "forever").</note>
**Q.** What is the sign?
**A.** While taking each other's hand, turn it over twice.

### *The Master’s Discourse.*

> You all know, my Brothers, that our Respectable Master Adonhiram <note>Adonhiram is a central figure in Masonic legend, often identified as the Master Architect of Solomon's Temple.</note> was massacred by the wickedness of three Fellowcrafts <note>original: "Compagnons." In the Masonic hierarchy, this is the second degree, ranking between Apprentice and Master.</note>; that one of them dealt him the death blow; and that all Masons must strive to find the murderer and punish him for his crime. Our Masters thus set out to search for him. One of them, after having crossed—with incredible hardship—the steepest mountains and the least-trodden paths, overwhelmed by fatigue, went to rest on the edge of a fountain called the Civi..... <note>Likely a reference to Siloam (Siloé), a site in Jerusalem mentioned on the previous page as the location where the assassin was found.</note> He perceived from afar a dark cave. Curiosity led him to go there. When he reached the entrance, he saw, deep within the recess, a faint light, by the glow of which he discovered a man lying in the same posture in which you see me now. (*He adopts the posture of a man overwhelmed by remorse.*) He asked him his name: he replied that he was called Abdacam <note>A variation of the name Abiram or Akirop, used in certain Masonic rituals to designate one of the three ruffians.</note>, and that he was fleeing the pursuit being made against him; that he was the murderer of Adonhiram, and that since that time he could find no refuge against the re- <note>The text cuts off here; it likely intended to say "remords" (remorse).</note>

<summary>This page contains a dramatic monologue delivered by the Master of the Lodge, detailing the arduous journey of a Master Mason who discovers one of Adonhiram's murderers, Abdacam, hiding in a cave near a fountain.</summary>
<keywords>Adonhiram, Abdacam, Masonic legend, Fellowcrafts, ritual discourse, pursuit of the assassins, Elect degrees</keywords>

At these words, the Master, overcome with rage, stepped forward and plunged a dagger into his breast, saying, "Sterkin," which means Vengeance <note>original: "Sterkin." This is a symbolic password used in the "Elect" degrees of Masonry. While its linguistic roots are debated, in this ritual context, it is explicitly defined as "Vengeance."</note>. Here, my Brothers, is the Institution of the class of Elect Masters <note>The "Elect" (Élu) degrees are a series of high-ranking Masonic levels that focus on the theme of bringing the murderers of the Master Architect to justice.</note>, which is a righteous continuation of Masonry, and the reason for which this Respectable Lodge has assembled today.

# THE LITTLE ARCHITECT.

->The Lodge Board.<-

This Board <note>The "Tableau" or Lodge Board (often called a Trestle Board) is a symbolic carpet or drawing placed on the floor in the center of the room, depicting the emblems of the degree.</note> is a long square <note>In Masonic terminology, a "long square" refers to a rectangle.</note>, drawn from the East to the West. At the end of the square in the West, there must be a triangle containing the Blazing Star, in which there is a letter G, and in the corners of the triangle one must see these three letters: S. V. G. <note>original: "S. V. G." These initials likely stand for *Sagesse, Vertu, Génie* (Wisdom, Virtue, Genius), which were virtues celebrated in this specific Masonic grade.</note> This triangle must be enclosed within a circle. The Lodge must be hung in black and lit by 21 lights, placed in sets of nine on two candlesticks <note>This accounts for 18 lights; the remaining three are mentioned later on the altar.</note>. The throne, which is in the East, must be raised on three steps; beside it, a small Altar will be placed, upon which shall be set a Bible, a Compass, a Square, a Triangle, a three-branched Candlestick for the three other lights, and an Urn containing a gold Trowel and a Paste made with milk, oil, wine, and flour. All the Brothers are placed as usual along the Board. Their Apron must be embroidered and lined with poppy-red fabric <term>Ponceau: a vivid, deep red color, specifically the hue of a wild poppy, often used in high-degree Masonic regalia.</term>. The Master and the Brothers wear at the

## O B L I G A T I O N.

> "I promise, upon the same **obligations** <term>obligations: the formal oaths or vows taken at each stage of Masonic advancement</term> that I have contracted in the previous **Degrees**, and before this august Assembly, to keep, guard, and hide the secrets of the **Architects**, to never reveal them to any Brother of the inferior Degrees, or to **Profanes** <term>Profanes: those who are not initiated into the secrets of Freemasonry</term>, under penalty of being deprived of the honorable burial which was granted to our Respectable Master <note>This refers to the legend of Hiram Abiff, whose remains were recovered and re-interred with honor after his murder.</note>. The other sign, which is for general use, is requested by placing the right hand over the heart, in the posture of the **Master’s sign** <note>The "Signe de Maître" is a fundamental Masonic gesture, usually involving the hand placed on the chest or throat to signify a penalty or oath.</note>, then tracing a diagonal line forward at the height of the face, and bringing it back to a horizontal position with the thumb resting on the forehead; this forms a **triangle**, then letting it fall back into the position of the Master’s sign. The response is to place the right hand flat on the right side, making a movement as if one wished to withdraw, while passing the right foot behind the left, which forms a **square** <note>original: "l’équerre"; referring to the architect's tool, a symbol of morality and rectitude in Masonry.</note>.

The **grip** <term>Grip: A ritualized handshake used to identify members of the same rank.</term> is given like that of a Master, by each quickly passing the hand under the elbow, which is taken in the palm of the hand, to pull each other with three jerks, pronouncing a syllable of the word <term>Gabaon</term> <note>original: "Gabaon"; likely referring to Gibeon, the "Great High Place" where Solomon offered sacrifices and where the Tabernacle was kept before the Temple was built.</note> each time. **Go now and make yourself known to all the Brothers, then you shall return to me.** This is executed. Upon his return, he says to him:

> My Brother, after the death of **Adonhiram** <term>Adonhiram: In certain Masonic traditions (Adonhiramite Masonry), this figure is the hero of the legend rather than Hiram Abiff, serving as the overseer of the works at the Temple.</term>, the works were proceeding sluggishly;

<sig>F 2</sig>

**are you an Architect?**

**Q. In what place were you received?** 
**A. Within the Circle of the quadrature and the** <term>Quadrature: Squaring the circle; a classic geometric problem that, in a Masonic context, symbolizes the attempt to reconcile the earthly (the square) with the divine (the circle).</term> 
**Holy of Holies.** <note>original: "Saint des Saints"; the innermost chamber of the Temple of Solomon, where the Ark of the Covenant was kept.</note>

**Q.** What does the Circle signify?
**A.** The immensity of the Grand Architect <note>A common Masonic designation for the Deity, reflecting the creator as the master builder of the universe.</note> who has neither beginning nor end.
**Q.** The Squaring? <note>original: "La Quadrature"; refers to the geometric problem of "squaring the circle," symbolizing the union of the earthly and the divine.</note>
**A.** The space of the <term>oblong square: A rectangular shape which, in Masonic tradition, represents the layout of the Temple and the Lodge.</term> of Adonhiram’s tomb.
**Q.** The Holy of Holies?
**A.** The space within the triangle where the name of the Grand Architect is engraved.
**Q.** By what means were you received as an Architect?
**A.** By the cement that was used to bind the stones of the Temple.
**Q.** What cement was used?
**A.** A mystic cement, composed of milk, oil, wine, and flour.
**Q.** What is the meaning of this mixture?
**A.** Milk represents sweetness, oil wisdom, wine strength, and flour goodness, which were the respectable qualities of our Master.
**Q.** How was he buried?
**A.** Facing toward the center, his mouth on the letter G, engraved in the <term>Blazing Star: An important Masonic symbol (Étoile Flamboyante) often representing the sun, divine light, or the human soul.</term> <note>The letter G typically stands for Geometry or God.</note>
**Q.** In what place was he buried?
**A.** Behind the sanctuary.
**Q.** Did Solomon leave the death of Adonhiram unpunished?
**A.** No. He appointed as Architects those who

# THE GRAND ARCHITECT,
### O R
# SCOTTISH FELLOWCRAFT.

### *Layout of the Lodge.*

To confer these Degrees <note>Referring to the sequence of "High Degrees" beyond the initial three.</note> successively, the lodge must be divided into two parts by a black curtain which must be behind the Master’s Throne. Beside it, there must be a small altar upon which is a Bible, a double triangle <note>Likely a Hexagram or Star of David, a common symbol in the "Architect" degrees representing the union of opposites or divine geometry.</note>, a square, a compass, and a tracing board <note>original: "planche à tracer"; a board or cloth where the symbols of the degree are drawn for the candidate to study.</note>, upon which the Temple of Solomon is drawn, raised to the beginning of the third story.

The entire Lodge must be draped in red, scattered with hyacinth flowers <note>original: "fleurs d’hyacinthe"; in 18th-century ritual texts, this likely refers to a specific shade of deep purplish-red or a stylized floral pattern associated with the biblical descriptions of the Tabernacle's hangings.</note>; but the wall-hanging of the first part must be covered by a black hanging, and arranged in such a way that it can disappear when the time comes. This first part must be lit by twenty-seven lights, placed by nines in three chandeliers. The titles of the Master and the <term>Wardens: Senior officers of the Lodge who assist the Master in his duties.</term> <note>original: "Surveillans"</note>, in the first

## J E W E L.

It is a double triangle <note>Formed by the interlacing of tools or geometric shapes, often creating a six-pointed star.</note> formed by a compass and a level, and enclosed within a golden circle. The head of the compass must be a golden sun, whose rays touch the head of the level. This Jewel hangs at the bottom of a wide red sash <term>Cordon: A ceremonial ribbon or sash worn over the shoulder as a mark of rank.</term> worn from left to right. It must be noted that, in the first Apartment draped in black, this Jewel must hang at the bottom of a black sash; and at the moment the wall-hangings change, the sash must also be changed.

### *Second Apartment.*

Behind the black curtain, there must be an Altar decorated with the attributes of Masonry, whether sculpted or painted. It must have seven steps, and must be surrounded by 81 lights, arranged in a triangle. Above it, there must be a transparency <note>original: "tableau en transparent"; a painting on thin fabric or glass illuminated from behind to create a glowing effect, a popular spectacle in 18th-century rituals.</note> representing the Glory of the Grand Architect, surrounded by the seven celestial Intelligences <note>In esoteric tradition, these are the seven planetary spirits or archangels who govern the cosmos.</note>; in the center shall be a luminous triangle, bearing the name of God written in Hebrew <note>original: "Hébreu"; likely referring to the Tetragrammaton, the four-letter name of God, יהוה.</note>. --- Page 85 ---
<meta>This page continues the formal reception of the Candidate into the rank of Grand Architect. Following the restoration of his sight on page 78, he is now being taught the secret modes of recognition—signs, grips, and passwords—necessary to prove his status to other members of the order.</meta>

<page-num>79</page-num>

"The sign of request consists of placing both hands on the stomach and forming a triangle there with the thumb and index finger of each hand; it is called the sign of call <note>original: "signe d'appel"</note>.

"The sign of response is to place both hands in the same shape above the head. This should only be used within the Lodge <note>The room or assembly where Masons meet</note> or in times of great necessity. It is called the sign of distress <note>original: "signe de secours"; literally "sign of help"</note>.

"The grip <note>original: "attouchement"; a specific way of shaking hands to recognize a fellow member</note> is performed by taking each other’s right hand and turning them over three times, one on top and then the other, alternately.

"The word is **Moabon** <note>A symbolic word in high-degree Masonry, often interpreted as "procreation of the father" or "from the father," and linked to the legend of the building of the Temple</note>. This word must only be spoken in syllables while turning the hands during the grip. Afterward, one shares an embrace <note>original: "l'accolade"; a fraternal kiss or hug common in French Masonry</note>.

"The password is **Schibboleth** <note>original: "Schibboleth"; a Hebrew word from the Book of Judges meaning "ear of grain" or "flowing stream," used historically as a test to identify members of a specific group</note>. Go, my Brother <note>original: "Frere"</note>, make yourself known to all the Brothers, and give them the signs, the word, the grip, and the embrace, and then return to give them back to me."

When he has given the signs, the grip, and the word to the Master <note>original: "Maître"; the presiding officer of the Lodge</note>, the latter says to him: "My Brother, proceed to the head of the Lodge, and pay attention to what is about to be said."

<meta>This page continues the initiation ritual for the Grand Architect grade, moving from the physical actions of the reception to the formal catechism—a question-and-answer session used to test a member’s knowledge of the degree’s symbolism and history.</meta>

<page-num>80</page-num>

### Scottish Companion’s Catechism. <note>original: "Catechisme de Compagnon Ecossois"; the "Scottish" degrees in Freemasonry refer to a system of higher grades that elaborate on the building of Solomon's Temple.</note>

Q. Most Respectable Senior Warden <note>The Senior Warden is one of the principal officers of a Masonic Lodge, responsible for overseeing the members during their labor.</note>
are you a Grand Architect?
A. Yes, Puissant Master <note>original: "Puissant Maître"; a title of high honor for the presiding officer.</note>, I have worked
in the third Apartment.
Q. Where were you received?
A. In the middle chamber. <note>A symbolic location within Solomon’s Temple where, according to Masonic tradition, Fellow Crafts received their wages and instructions.</note>
Q. What is this Chamber?
A. The place where Solomon worked on the
plan of the Temple with the Grand Archi-
tect.
Q. At what do you occupy yourself in the
Grade of Grand Architect?
A. At building the final structure or the third
section <note>original: "corps"; literally "body" or "part" of a building.</note> which forms the summit of the build-
ing.
Q. What made you deserve this Grade?
A. The perfection of the drawings that I pre-
sented to Solomon.
Q. What was your reward?
A. Two signs, a grip <note>original: "attouchement"; a secret handshake.</note> and two
words.
Q. Give them to me?
A. Puissant Master, give me the first
one, and I will give you the second.
->( They are exchanged. )<-

--- Page 87 ---
<meta>This page continues the formal "Catechism" or ritual question-and-answer session for the Grand Architect degree. Following the exchange of signs on the previous page, the Master now asks for their names and symbolic meanings, as well as the passwords associated with this rank.</meta>

<page-num>( 81 )</page-num>

Q. What do you call these signs?
A. The first is called the sign of inquiry <note>original: "signe de demande"; used to request recognition or information</note>, and the other the sign of distress <note>original: "signe de secours"; used to call for aid</note>.
Q. What does this latter sign demonstrate, and why do you call it so?
A. That a good Mason <note>original: "Maçon"</note> must count firmly on the friendship and the aid of his Brothers. Therefore, one must not make this sign outside of the Lodge <note>original: "Loge"</note> except in pressing dangers.
Q. Give the grip <term>Grip: A specific ritual touch or handshake used to identify a member's rank in the fraternity without speaking</term> to the Second Brother?
->( It is given, while saying *Moabon*. )<- <note>Moabon is a ritual password found in several Masonic degrees; its meaning is often associated with the phrase "the son of the father" or linked to the discovery of a body in the Hiram Abiff legend</note>
Q. What is the word you have just pronounced?
A. The word that was given to me by Solomon <note>King Solomon, the traditional founder of the Masonic craft</note>.
Q. What is the other word?
A. *Shibboleth* <note>A Hebrew word meaning an ear of corn or a flowing stream; biblically, it was used as a password to identify members of a specific tribe (Judges 12:6)</note>, which is the password.
Q. How old are you? <note>In Masonic rituals, "age" represents the symbolic level of knowledge attained rather than actual years lived</note>
A. Twenty-seven years.
The Puissant <note>original: "Le Puissant"; the title for the presiding officer in this degree</note> to the Wardens <note>original: "Surveillans"; the officers who assist the Master in governing the lodge</note>: Respectable Brothers, Senior and Junior Wardens, announce, each on your side, that I am going to close the Lodge of the Grand Architect.
->( They do so. )<-
Q. What time is it, Brother Senior Warden?

**R.** <note>Abbreviation for Response</note> Puissant Master, this is the last moment, the last hour, the last day that the Grand Architect of the Universe <note>original: "Grand Architecte de l'Univers"; a Masonic title for God, reflecting the degree's focus on architecture and divine design</note> was occupied with the creation of the world. This is the last moment, the last hour, the last day that Solomon <note>King Solomon, who in Masonic tradition is the first Grand Master</note> employed in building the Temple.

# THE SCOTTISH MASTER. <note>original: "Le Maitre Ecossois"; this represents the Sixth Degree in this specific Masonic rite, building upon the architectural themes of the previous levels.</note>

## *Layout of the Lodge.*

It has been seen, at the beginning of the Grand Architect or Scottish Fellow <note>original: "Compagnon Ecossois"; the term "Fellow" or "Fellowcraft" refers to the second stage of Masonic progression, here adapted into the higher Scottish degrees</note>, in what manner the Room located behind the black curtain at the back must be decorated. As for the first room, it is the same as in the preceding Grade. One must only take care to bring back to the West <note>original: "l'Occident"; the station of the Wardens, symbolizing the close of day and the place where laborers receive their wages</note>, between the two Wardens, the small Altar that is near the Throne; and in place of the first tracing board <note>original: "Planche à tracer"; a diagram or illustration used to teach the symbolic lessons of the degree</note>, another must be placed that represents Solomon's Temple totally finished, but without any ornament.

At the bottom of this board, several tools must have been drawn, and behind it, the names of all the Brothers who are to pass through this Grade must be visible, which they must have written themselves; and at the end of these names, a space must remain to insert the word **EXCLUDED** <note>original: "EXCLUS"</note>; seeing as if, at the moment of reception, someone were to withdraw, they would effectively be excluded in perpetuity. In front of the small Altar, one must place a

### *Titles and Opening of the Lodge.*

In a Lodge of a Scottish Master, the Worshipful Master <note>original: "Vénérable"; the presiding officer</note> is called Thrice Puissant, the Wardens <note>original: "Surveillans"</note> Thrice Respectable, and all the Brothers Thrice Honorable. This Lodge opens in the same manner as the preceding one. Then, the Thrice Puissant commands the Wardens to ensure that all is well tiled <note>original: "bien couvert"; a Masonic term meaning the room is secured from eavesdroppers or unauthorized persons</note>, and if all Brothers present are Scottish Masters; and after the Wardens have answered the Thrice Puissant, the latter asks them several questions from the Catechism <note>original: "Catéchisme"; a formal series of questions and answers used to test a Mason's knowledge</note>, while waiting for the Candidate to be ready.

### *Preparation and Reception.*

After the Lodge of the Scottish Fellow <note>original: "Compagnon Ecossois"; the degree immediately preceding this one</note> is closed, the new Proselyte <note>term: "Proselyte" - a person who has recently joined a group or, in this context, someone being initiated into a higher degree</note> is led into the Room of Lost Steps <note>original: "Chambre des Pas-perdus"; a traditional name for an antechamber or waiting hall</note>. There, he is placed in the same state he was in for the previous Reception, except that his eyes are not blindfolded. The Master of Ceremonies gives him a moral discourse on Masonry in general and on the Grade he is about to receive, then to him...

» My Brother, although you have passed through all the Degrees <note>French: *Grades*. In Masonry, these are the hierarchical steps or levels of initiation.</note>, you have not yet reached perfection <note>In this context, "perfection" refers to the completion of Masonic knowledge and the moral refinement of the initiate.</note>. It remains for us to reveal to you that brilliant light which Masonry promises you in all its Degrees, and which is finally granted only after many trials. There are still difficult ones to endure. It is for you to decide; we are still ready to release you from your promises, even those you have made with us. The fear of seeing them profaned <note>Meaning the secrets being revealed to the uninitiated or treated without proper respect.</note> would not stop us. We require a different commitment from all those you have already taken. Those occurred in darkness <note>A reference to earlier degrees where the candidate is often symbolically blindfolded or kept in ignorance of the ritual's full meaning until the end.</note>. This one must take place in full liberty. You may even read it yourself. It is written on this paper presented to you. Read it quietly. If you consent to it, you will repeat it aloud.

### O B L I G A T I O N

» By all the liberty I profess through all five natural senses, by the existence of my reason and my spirit—which I declare to be in no way enslaved—and by the intelligence that sustains, guides, and enlightens me. Yes, I promise, I swear, I make a vow to keep inviolably

pronounces **CAKI** <note>A ritual word. <meta>This page continues the initiation ceremony for the Grade of Superintendent Scottish Master. Having been purified with water and invested with the sash and jewel, the candidate is now entrusted with the secret recognition signs, grips, and passwords of the degree.</meta>

<page-num>93</page-num>

"In this Degree, to recognize one another, we have two signs, a grip <note>French: *attouchement*. A specific way of shaking hands or touching used to verify a member's rank.</note>, and four words.

The first two are *Urim* and *Thummim* <note>original: *Urim* and *Thumin*. These are Hebrew terms referring to elements of the High Priest's breastplate in the Old Testament, often associated with seeking divine revelation.</note>.

The sacred and incommunicable word is *Jehovah* <note>original: *Jéhova*. In Masonic tradition, the "True Name of God," which was central to the myths surrounding the building of Solomon's Temple.</note>, the word of the ancient Masters which has never been lost.

The password is *Zedidiac* <note>Likely a variation of *Jedidiah*, the name given to King Solomon by the prophet Nathan, meaning "Beloved of the Lord."</note>.

The sign is to present the hands while forming a triangle at the height of the forehead, while saying: '*Triangular at the forehead, this is my point of support*.'

One responds to this sign by placing the right hand over the eyes, bowing the head, and bending the knee.

The grip <note>French: *attouchement*</note> is to take each other’s right hands as in the degree of Grand Architect; but, instead of turning them over three times, one mutually gives three small taps with the four fingers held together, while placing the left hand on the right shoulder; then one embraces, saying: '*Virtue unites two hearts, two bodies, two hands, and all these make but one*.' Now, my Brother, go and return these honorable marks of a true Mason <note>French: *Maçon*</note> to the Most Respectable Wardens <note>French: *Surveillans*. Senior officers who sit in the West of the lodge.</note>, and you shall then return near the Throne <note>The Master's ceremonial chair at the East of the lodge.</note> to hear the Instruction."

<meta>This page transitions from the physical ritual actions described on the previous page to the formal catechism—a series of questions and answers used to test a Mason's knowledge of the degree's symbolism and legendary history.
